A: ["Melanie Klein", "Object relations theory", "Relational theory", "Object theory", "Schema therapy", "Homotopy type theory"]
A: Schema therapy was developed by Jeffrey E. Young for use in treatment of personality disorders and chronic DSM Axis I disorders, such as when patients fail to respond or relapse after having been through other therapies (for example, traditional cognitive behavioral therapy).
A: Exit? No.
A: ["Melanie Klein", "Object relations theory", "Relational theory", "Object theory", "Schema therapy", "Homotopy type theory", "Interpersonal Cognitive Behavioral Therapy", "Mindfulness-based cognitive therapy", "Cognitive processing therapy", "Exposure therapy", "Association for Behavioral and Cognitive Therapies"]
A: Schema therapy was developed by Jeffrey E. Young for use in treatment of personality disorders and chronic DSM Axis I disorders, such as when patients fail to respond or relapse after having been through other therapies (for example, traditional cognitive behavioral therapy). So the answer is: Schema therapy.

A: ["Boston Christmas Tree", "Ainslie Glen, Nova Scotia", "MacDonalds Glen, Nova Scotia", "Pine Tree, Nova Scotia", "Glen Haven, Nova Scotia", "Christmas Island, Nova Scotia", "Christmas tree", "Chicago Christmas Tree", "Artificial Christmas tree", "Vatican Christmas Tree", "Mount Ingino Christmas Tree"]
A: The Boston Christmas Tree is the City of Boston, Massachusetts' official Christmas tree. A tree has been lit each year since 1941, and since 1971 it has been given to the people of Boston by the people of Nova Scotia in thanks for their assistance after the 1917 Halifax Explosion.

A: ["Boston Christmas Tree", "Ainslie Glen, Nova Scotia", "MacDonalds Glen, Nova Scotia", "Pine Tree, Nova Scotia", "Glen Haven, Nova Scotia", "Christmas Island, Nova Scotia", "Christmas tree", "Chicago Christmas Tree", "Artificial Christmas tree", "Vatican Christmas Tree", "Mount Ingino Christmas Tree", "Halifax Explosion (disambiguation)", "Boston\u2013Halifax relations", "Has Been", "Halifax Explosion"]

So the answer is: in thanks for their assistance after the 1917 Halifax Explosion.
